I’m g‑going mad﻿—I’m g‑going m‑mad! I’m d‑d‑done for.﻿ ﻿…”
“All right, old fellow﻿—just wait till I strike a light!”
“Oh, Taffy! I haven’t slept for four nights﻿—not a wink! She d‑d‑died with Sv﻿—Sv﻿—Sv﻿ ﻿… damn it, I can’t get it out! that ruffian’s name on her lips!﻿ ﻿… it was just as if he were calling her from the t‑t‑tomb! She recovered her senses the very minute she saw his photograph﻿—she was so f‑fond of him she f‑forgot everybody else! She’s gone straight to him, after all﻿—in some other life!﻿ ﻿… to slave for him, and sing for him, and help him to make better music than ever! Oh, T﻿—T﻿—oh﻿—oh! Taffy﻿—oh! oh! oh! catch hold! c‑c‑catch.﻿ ﻿…” And Little Billee had all but fallen on the floor in a fit.
And all the old miserable business of five years before had begun over again!
There has been too much sickness in this story, so I will tell as little as possible of poor Little Billee’s long illness, his slow and only partial recovery, the paralysis of his powers as a painter, his quick decline, his early death, his manly, calm, and most beautiful surrender﻿—the wedding of the moth with the star, of the night with the morrow!
For all but blameless as his short life had been, and so full of splendid promise and performance, nothing ever became him better than the way he left it. It was as if he were starting on some distant holy quest, like some gallant knight of old﻿—“A Bagot to the Rescue!” It shook the infallibility of a certain vicar down to its very foundations, and made him think more deeply about things than he had ever thought yet. It gave him pause!﻿ ﻿… and so wrung his heart that when, at the last, he stooped to kiss his poor young dead friend’s pure white forehead, he dropped a bigger tear on it than Little Billee (once so given to the dropping of big tears) had ever dropped in his life.
But it is all too sad to write about.
It was by Little Billee’s bedside, in Devonshire, that Taffy had grown to love Blanche Bagot, and not very many weeks after it was all over that Taffy had asked her to be his wife; and in a year they were married, and a very happy marriage it turned out﻿—the one thing that poor Mrs. Bagot still looks upon as a compensation for all the griefs and troubles of her life.
During the first year or two Blanche had perhaps been the most ardently loving of this well-assorted pair.

“噢，塔菲！我已经连续整整四个通宵没合过眼了——连一下都没合上！她是嘴里念叨着斯——斯——斯……见鬼，我连这个名字都说不出口！她死时嘴唇里念叨着那个恶棍的名字！……就仿佛他在坟墓里召、召唤她一样！就在看见那张照片的一瞬间，她彻底找回了神智——她是那么深、深爱着他，把旁的所有人全都忘、忘得一干二净！到头来，她终究还是径直奔向他去了——在另一个世界里！……去伺候他，去为他歌唱，去帮他谱写比以往更美妙的音乐！噢，塔——塔——噢——噢！塔菲——噢！噢！噢！抱住我！快、快抱住我。……”说着，小比利发作般一头瘫倒在地，剧烈抽搐起来。
于是，五年前那场令人心碎的凄惨噩梦，再度不可逆转地卷土重来！
在这部故事里已经充斥了太多的病痛与哀伤，因此对于可怜小比利接下来的漫长病榻岁月、那缓慢而微弱的局部好转、作为画家全部才华的丧失殆尽、那急转直下的生命陨落、那英年早逝，以及他那坦然、平静而绝美动人的告别谢幕，我不愿再多费笔墨赘述——那宛若飞蛾扑向星辰的壮烈结合，黑夜与黎明的相拥！
因为尽管他这短暂的一生几乎毫无瑕疵，充满了辉煌的才华与成就，然而世间再无任何事物比他辞世时的风范更为庄严高贵。他宛如古代那些英勇无畏的骑士踏上某段遥远神圣的征程——“巴戈特骑士前来驰援救赎！”这彻底动摇了某位牧师先生原本自命不凡的正统信仰根基，逼得他平生头一次去深思世事的本质。这让他陷入了良久的沉思与反省！……以至于当最终俯下身去亲吻这位可怜逝去青年额前纯洁苍白的肌肤时，牧师眼中滚落的泪珠，比小比利自己生前（那个曾经动辄泪流满面的少年）平生掉过的任何眼泪都要硕大。
然而这一切实在太凄楚感伤，教人不忍落笔。
正是在德文郡陪伴在小比利病榻旁的日日夜夜里，塔菲深深爱上了布兰奇·巴戈特；在这场悲剧谢幕后没过多少周，塔菲便向她郑重求婚；一年后他们喜结连理，事实证明这是一段无比美满的姻缘——这成了可怜的巴戈特太太心底认为能够补偿自身平生全部悲痛与苦难的唯一慰藉。

===Original===

That beautiful look of love surprised (which makes all women’s eyes look the same) came into hers whenever she looked at Taffy, and filled his heart with tender compunction, and a queer sense of his own unworthiness.
Then a boy was born to them, and that look fell on the boy, and the good Taffy caught it as it passed him by, and he felt a helpless, absurd jealousy, that was none the less painful for being so ridiculous! and then that look fell on another boy and yet another, so that it was through these boys that she looked at their father. Then *his* eyes caught the look, and kept it for their own use; and he grew never to look at his wife without it; and as no daughter came, she retained for life the monopoly of that most sweet and expressive regard.
They are not very rich. He is a far better sportsman than he will ever be a painter; and if he doesn’t sell his pictures, it is not because they are too good for the public taste: indeed, he has no illusions on that score himself, even if his wife has! He is quite the least conceited art-duffer I ever met﻿—and I have met many far worse duffers than Taffy.
Would only that I might kill off his cousin Sir Oscar, and Sir Oscar’s five sons (the Wynnes are good at sons), and his seventeen grandsons, and the fourteen cousins (and their numerous male progeny), that stand between Taffy and the baronetcy, and whatever property goes with it, so that he might be Sir Taffy, and dear Blanche Bagot (that was) might be called “my lady”! This Shakespearian holocaust would scarcely cost me a pang!
It is a great temptation, when you have duly slain your first hero, to enrich hero number two beyond the dreams of avarice, and provide him with a title and a castle and park, as well as a handsome wife and a nice family! But truth is inexorable﻿—and, besides, they are just as happy as they are.
They are well off enough, anyhow, to spend a week in Paris at last, and even to stop at the Grand Hôtel! now that two of their sons are at Harrow (where their father was before them), and the third is safe at a preparatory school at Elstree, Herts.
It is their first outing since the honeymoon, and the Laird should have come with them.
But the good Laird of Cockpen (who is now a famous Royal Academician) is preparing for a honeymoon of his own.

===Chinese===

每当她凝视塔菲时，眼眸中总会浮现出那种因意外惊喜而焕发的动人爱意神采（那神采让天下所有女人的眼眸都变得这般美丽），让塔菲心头充盈着温柔的内疚与一种自觉配不上她的奇异自惭。
随后他们迎来了一个男婴，那充满爱意的目光便落在了男孩身上，好心肠的塔菲捕获了这从身旁掠过的目光，心中竟不由得泛起一股无助而荒唐的嫉妒，正因其荒诞不经反而愈发教人难受！随后那目光又落在了第二个男孩身上，继而又落在了第三个男孩身上，以至于她是透过这一群儿子在凝视着他们的父亲。接着，塔菲的双眸也学会了这种眼神，据为己用；从此他看妻子时再也离不开这般深情；而由于一直未添女儿，她终生独占着这最温柔动人的深情注视。
他们家境算不上大富大贵。塔菲是个远比画家更出色的猎手；即便他的画作销路平平，也绝非因为艺术水准太高超以致曲高和寡：事实上，即便妻子对他盲目崇拜，他自己在这桩事上却毫无幻想！在我所结识的画坛庸手之中，他是最不自命不凡的一位——而且我见过比塔菲糟糕得多的庸手。
真巴不得我能大笔一挥，除掉他的堂兄奥斯卡爵士，以及奥斯卡爵士的五个儿子（韦恩家族向来人丁兴旺）、十七个孙子和十四位堂兄弟（以及他们无数的男性后裔）——正是这浩浩荡荡的继承队伍横亘在塔菲与从男爵爵位及家族领地之间——好让他名正言顺获封塔菲爵士，让我们亲爱的布兰奇·巴戈特小姐得以尊称一声“夫人”！这场莎士比亚式的血腥大屠杀绝不会让我皱一下眉头！
在依法除掉了第一位男主角之后，作者难免极受诱惑去让第二位男主角富得流油，不仅赐给他绝色娇妻与美满家庭，还兼备贵族头衔、城堡与广袤庄园！然而事实是无情的——更何况，他们如今的生活早已足够幸福。
无论如何，他们的境况已足够宽裕，如今终于能在巴黎惬意度上一周，甚至下榻在奢华的大旅馆！如今他们的两个儿子都在哈罗公学就读（当年他们的父亲亦在此就学），小儿子则安然就读于赫特福德郡埃尔斯特里的预备学校。
这是他们自蜜月以来的头一回远行，原本领主大人也该与他们结伴同游。
然而可敬的科克彭领主大人（如今已是声名赫赫的皇家艺术研究院院士）此刻正忙着张罗他自己的蜜月呢。

===Original===

He has gone to Scotland to be married himself﻿—to wed a fair and clever countrywoman of just a suitable age, for he has known her ever since she was a bright little lassie in short frocks, and he a promising A.R.A. (the pride of his native Dundee)﻿—a marriage of reason, and well-seasoned affection, and mutual esteem﻿—and therefore sure to turn out a happy one! and in another fortnight or so the pair of them will very possibly be sitting to breakfast opposite each other at that very corner table in the courtyard of the Grand Hôtel! and she will laugh at everything he says﻿—and they will live happily ever after.
So much for hero number three﻿—D’Artagnan! Here’s to you, Sandy McAlister, canniest, genialest, and most humorous of Scots! most delicate, and dainty, and fanciful of British painters! “I trink your health, mit your family’s﻿—may you lif long﻿—and brosper!”
---
So Taffy and his wife have come for their second honeymoon, their Indian-summer honeymoon, alone; and are well content that it should be so. Two’s always company for such a pair﻿—the amusing one and the amusable!﻿—and they are making the most of it!
They have been all over the quartier latin, and revisited the well-remembered spots; and even been allowed to enter the old studio, through the kindness of the concierge (who is no longer Madame Vinard). It is tenanted by two American painters, who are coldly civil on being thus disturbed in the middle of their work.
The studio is very spick and span, and most respectable. Trilby’s foot, and the poem, and the sheet of plate-glass have been improved away, and a bookshelf put in their place. The new concierge (who has only been there a year) knows nothing of Trilby, and of the Vinards, only that they are rich and prosperous, and live somewhere in the south of France, and that Monsieur Vinard is mayor of his commune. *Que le bon Dieu les bénisse! c’étaient de bien braves gens.*
Then Mr. and Mrs. Taffy have also been driven (in an open calèche with two horses) through the Bois de Boulogne to St. Cloud; and to Versailles, where they lunched at the Hôtel des Réservoirs﻿—*parlez-moi de ça*! and to St.

===Chinese===

领主大人自己如今也赶赴苏格兰完婚去啦——迎娶了一位秀美温存、聪明伶俐且年岁相当的同乡才女；早在他初出茅庐刚当选皇家艺术研究院准会员（当年堪称故乡邓迪的骄傲）、而新娘尚是个穿短裙的小姑娘时，两人便已相识——这是一场建立在理智、岁月沉淀的深厚情义与彼此敬重之上的美满良缘——注定会是一段幸福的结合！再过大约两周，他们夫妇二人极可能也会面对面坐在大旅馆庭院那个拐角小桌旁享用早餐！而新娘会对他说的每一句话都报以欢快的笑声——从此他们将白头偕老、幸福美满。
关于第三位火枪手——达达尼昂——且说到此为止！敬你一杯，桑迪·麦卡利斯特，苏格兰人中最精明、豪爽、幽默的硬汉！英国画坛最细腻、雅致且富有奇思妙想的宗师！“敬你的健康，敬你的阖府安康——祝你长寿百岁——繁荣昌盛！”
---
于是塔菲同他的妻子独自踏上了这趟二次蜜月、这趟秋阳晚晴般的蜜月；两人对此满心欢喜。对于这样一对璧人而言，二人世界向来其乐融融——一个风趣逗人，一个极易被逗乐！——他们尽情撷取着每一分欢愉！
他们走遍了整个拉丁区，重温了每一处记忆犹新的旧迹；在看门人的行好通融下（如今已不再是维纳尔太太了），甚至获准踏进了当年的旧画室。如今里面租住着两名美国画家，对于在作画中途受到这般打扰，显得冷淡而客套。
画室收拾得极其整洁光鲜、体面规矩。特里尔比的那只脚、那首诗以及那块厚玻璃板，早已在历次装修翻新中荡然无存，原处如今立着一只书架。新来的看门人（才在此当差一年）对特里尔比一无所知；至于维纳尔夫妇，只听说他们如今腰缠万贯、飞黄腾达，定居在法国南部的某个地方，维纳尔先生如今已荣升为当地市镇的市长大人。愿好心肠的上帝保佑他们！他们当年可真是一对厚道的大好人。
随后，塔菲夫妇还乘着双马敞篷大马车，穿过布洛涅森林前往圣克卢；又游历了凡尔赛宫，并在水库大旅馆用了午餐——那排场真是没得说！接着又去了圣日耳曼，以及默东

===Original===

Germain, and to Meudon (where they lunched at *la loge du garde champêtre*﻿—a new one); they have visited the Salon, the Louvre, the porcelain manufactory at Sèvres, the Gobelins, the Hôtel Cluny, the Invalides, with Napoleon’s tomb, and seen half a dozen churches, including Notre Dame and the Sainte Chapelle; and dined with the Dodors at their charming villa near Asnières, and with the Zouzous at the splendid Hôtel de la Rochemartel, and with the Duriens in the Parc Monceau (Dodor’s food was best and Zouzou’s worst; and at Durien’s the company and talk were so good that one forgot to notice the food﻿—and that was a pity). And the young Dodors are all right﻿—and so are the young Duriens. As for the young Zouzous, there aren’t any﻿—and that’s a relief.
And they’ve been to the Variétés and seen Madame Chaumont, and to the Français and seen Sarah Bernhardt and Côquelin and Delaunay, and to the Opéra and heard Monsieur Lassalle.
And today being their last day, they are going to laze and flane about the boulevards, and buy things, and lunch anywhere, *sur le pouce*, and do the Bois once more and see *tout Paris*, and dine early at Durand’s, or Bignon’s (or else the Café des Ambassadeurs), and finish up the well-spent day at the Mouches d’Espagne﻿—the new theatre in the Boulevard Poissonnière﻿—to see Madame Cantharidi in *Petits Bonheurs de Contrebande*, which they are told is immensely droll and quite proper﻿—funny without being vulgar! Dodor was their informant﻿—he had taken Madame Dodor to see it three or four times.
Madame Cantharidi, as everybody knows, is a very clever but extremely plain old woman with a cracked voice﻿—of spotless reputation, and the irreproachable mother of a grown-up family whom she has brought up in perfection. They have never been allowed to see their mother (and grandmother) act﻿—not even the sons. Their excellent father (who adores both them and her) has drawn the line at that!
In private life she is “quite the lady,” but on the stage﻿—well, go and see her, and you will understand how she comes to be the idol of the Parisian public. For she is the true and liberal dispenser to them of that modern *esprit gaulois* which would make the good Rabelais turn uneasily in his grave and blush there like a Benedictine Sister.
And truly she deserves the reverential love and gratitude of her *chers Parisiens*!

===Chinese===

在默东，他们在田园卫士哨所旧址上新建的餐馆用了午餐；他们参观了官方沙龙画展、卢浮宫、塞夫尔瓷器厂、戈布兰挂毯厂、克吕尼博物馆，以及安放着拿破仑灵柩的荣军院，还参拜了半打著名的教堂，包括巴黎圣母院与圣礼拜堂；他们还同多多尔夫妇在阿涅尔附近迷人的乡间别墅共进晚餐，同祖祖夫妇在里尔街金碧辉煌的罗什马泰尔府邸欢聚，并在蒙梭公园同迪里安夫妇把酒言欢（在各家中，多多尔家的菜肴最可口，祖祖家的饭菜最寒碜；而在迪里安家，满座高朋的谈吐风趣至极，以至于大家甚至忘记了品评菜肴的滋味——这倒委实有点可惜）。小多多尔们个个活泼健康——小迪里安们也一样聪明可爱。至于小祖祖们嘛，一个也没生出来——这倒让人松了一大口气。
他们还去了杂耍剧院观看了绍蒙夫人的表演，去了法兰西喜剧院欣赏了萨拉·伯恩哈特、科克兰与德洛奈的精湛演技，并在歌剧院聆听了拉萨尔先生的动人歌喉。
今天正是他们在巴黎逗留的最后一天，他们打算在林荫大道上闲散漫游、随意购物，在街边小馆随性解决午餐，再去布洛涅森林兜上一圈看全巴黎的豪门名流，傍晚在迪朗餐馆或比尼翁餐馆（抑或使节咖啡馆）早早用完晚膳，最后前往鱼贩林荫大道上新建的“西班牙苍蝇”剧场，观赏坎塔丽迪夫人的成名大作《走私来的小确幸》，以此圆满结束这充实的一天。据多多尔介绍，这出戏极其滑稽逗趣且极为正派得体——诙谐幽默却丝毫不显下流庸俗！多多尔正是他们的情报官——他自己已经陪着多多尔太太连看了三四遍。
正如尽人皆知的那样，坎塔丽迪夫人是一位才华横溢却相貌极其平庸丑陋、嗓音沙哑破损的老妪——在私德上却无可挑剔、守身如玉，是一位把膝下子女调教得十全十美的模范母亲。她的子女甚至孙辈们生前从未被允许踏进剧场亲眼看母亲演戏——连儿子们也不例外。他们那位可敬的父亲（深爱着妻子与孩子们）在这一桩事上划下了绝对不可逾越的底线！
在私人生活里，她是十足体面的“高贵淑女”，然而一旦登上了舞台——咳，只要亲自去看上一回，你便能彻底懂得她何以会成为全巴黎公众如痴如醉的偶像。因为唯有她，才是当代高卢精神毫不吝啬的真正化身；那种泼辣戏谑的风趣劲儿，足以让可敬的拉伯雷在棺材板里不安地翻过身来，羞涩得宛如本笃会修道院里的见习修女。
她委实当之无愧地赢得了她那些“亲爱的巴黎市民”由衷的崇拜与感恩！

===Original===

She amused them all through the Empire; during the *année terrible* she was their only stay and comfort, and has been their chief delight ever since, and is now.
When they come back from *La Revanche*, may Madame Cantharidi be still at her post, “Les mouches d’Espagne,” to welcome the returning heroes, and exult and crow with them in her funny cracked old voice; or, haply, even console them once more, as the case may be.
“Victors or vanquished, they will laugh the same!”
Mrs. Taffy is a poor French scholar. One must know French very well indeed (and many other things besides) to seize the subtle points of Madame Cantharidi’s play (and byplay)!
But Madame Cantharidi has so droll a face and voice, and such very droll, odd movements that Mrs. Taffy goes into fits of laughter as soon as the quaint little old lady comes on the stage. So heartily does she laugh that a good Parisian bourgeois turns round and remarks to his wife: “*V’là une jolie p’tite Anglaise qui n’est pas bégueule, an moins! Et l’ gros bœuf avec les yeux bleus en boules de loto﻿—c’est son mari, sans doute! il n’a pas l’air trop content par exemple, celui-là!*”
The fact is that the good Taffy (who knows French very well indeed) is quite scandalized, and very angry with Dodor for sending them there; and as soon as the first act is finished he means, without any fuss, to take his wife away.
As he sits patiently, too indignant to laugh at what is really funny in the piece (much of it is vulgar *without* being funny), he finds himself watching a little white-haired man in the orchestra, a fiddler, the shape of whose back seems somehow familiar, as he plays an obbligato accompaniment to a very broadly comic song of Madame Cantharidi’s. He plays beautifully﻿—like a master﻿—and the loud applause is as much for him as for the vocalist.
Presently this fiddler turns his head so that his profile can be seen, and Taffy recognizes him.
After five minutes’ thought, Taffy takes a leaf out of his pocketbook and writes (in perfectly grammatical French):

> “Dear Gecko﻿—You have not forgotten Taffy Wynne, I hope; and Litrebili, and Litrebili’s sister, who is now Mrs. Taffy Wynne. We leave Paris tomorrow, and would like very much to see you once more. Will you, after the play, come and sup with us at the Café Anglais? If so, look up and make ‘yes’ with the head, and enchant
> “Your well-devoted Taffy Wynne.”

===Chinese===

在整个第二帝国时期，她的滑稽戏逗乐了全巴黎；在那个惨烈的“恐怖之年”里，她是巴黎人唯一的精神支柱与抚慰；从那以后直到如今，她始终是人们最大的欢乐源泉。
待到巴黎人哪天从“复仇之战”中凯旋归来，但愿坎塔丽迪夫人依然坚守在“西班牙苍蝇”剧场的岗位上，用她那滑稽破损的老嗓子迎候归来的英雄们，同他们一起欢欣雀跃、引吭高歌；抑或在必要时，再次温柔地抚慰他们受创的心灵。
“无论胜败，他们总是这般开怀大笑！”
塔菲太太的法语水平实在不敢恭维。须知，想要彻底领悟坎塔丽迪夫人台词与即兴表演中的微妙妙趣，法语必须极其精通才行！
然而坎塔丽迪夫人的长相与腔调实在太滑稽怪诞了，加之那些滑稽奇特的肢体动作，以至于这位古怪的小老太太刚一登台，塔菲太太便笑得前仰后合。她笑得那般发自肺腑、毫无顾忌，以至于旁边一位地道的巴黎小市民忍不住转过身来对妻子嘀咕道：“瞧瞧，好一位不装腔作势的漂亮英国小娘子！旁边那个长着弹珠般蓝眼睛的大笨牛想必就是她丈夫了；不过瞧他的脸色，倒显得不怎么乐意呢！”
事实正是如此，好心肠的塔菲（其法语极其地道流利）此刻感到被深深冒犯了，甚至对多多尔推荐他们来看这出戏感到极其恼怒；他打定主意等第一幕一拉上大幕，就悄无声息地带着妻子抽身离席。
当他强压怒火、耐着性子坐在一旁，对戏里真正滑稽的桥段冷眼旁观时（戏里许多段子纯属低俗下流却并不好笑），他的目光不由自主地被乐池里一位满头白发、身形瘦小的提琴手吸引住了。当那人为坎塔丽迪夫人的一首极其低俗诙谐的歌谣伴奏独奏华彩时，其后背的轮廓让塔菲感到莫名熟悉。那人拉得绝妙传神——宛如一代宗师——全场爆发出的雷鸣掌声，与其说是献给台上的歌者，毋宁说是献给这位卓越的提琴手。
不一会儿，这位提琴手微微侧过脸，露出了侧颜轮廓，塔菲一眼认出了他。
沉思了五分钟之后，塔菲从随身记事簿里撕下一页纸，用极其规范标准的法语写道：

> “亲爱的盖科——我深信你未曾忘记塔菲·韦恩；未曾忘记小比利，以及小比利的妹妹——如今已是塔菲·韦恩夫人。我们定于明日告别巴黎，极度渴望能与你再度重逢一面。演出结束后，你可愿赏光前往英国咖啡馆同我们共进宵夜？若蒙允诺，请抬起头朝上方微微颔首示意，以慰藉
> “你赤诚笃定的塔菲·韦恩。”

===Original===

He gives this, folded, to an attendant﻿—for *le premier violon﻿—celui qui a des cheveux blancs*.
Presently he sees Gecko receive the note and read it and ponder for a while.
Then Gecko looks round the theatre, and Taffy waves his handkerchief and catches the eye of the *premier violon*, who “makes ‘yes’ with the head.”
And then, the first act over, Mr. and Mrs. Wynne leave the theatre; Mr. explaining why, and Mrs. very ready to go, as she was beginning to feel strangely uncomfortable without quite realizing as yet what was amiss with the lively Madame Cantharidi.
They went to the Café Anglais and bespoke a nice little room on the entresol overlooking the boulevard, and ordered a nice little supper; salmi of something very good, mayonnaise of lobster, and one or two other dishes better still﻿—and chambertin of the best. Taffy was particular about these things on a holiday, and regardless of expense. Porthos was very hospitable, and liked good food and plenty of it; and Athos dearly loved good wine!
And then they went and sat at a little round table outside the Café de la Paix on the boulevard, near the Grand Opéra, where it is always very gay, and studied Paris life, and nursed their appetites till suppertime.
At half-past eleven Gecko made his appearance﻿—very meek and humble. He looked old﻿—ten years older than he really was﻿—much bowed down, and as if he had roughed it all his life, and had found living a desperate long, hard grind.
He kissed Mrs. Taffy’s hand, and seemed half inclined to kiss Taffy’s too, and was almost tearful in his pleasure at meeting them again, and his gratitude at being asked to sup with them. He had soft, clinging, caressing manners, like a nice dog’s, that made you his friend at once. He was obviously genuine and sincere, and quite pathetically simple, as he always had been.
At first he could scarcely eat for nervous excitement; but Taffy’s fine example and Mrs. Taffy’s genial, easygoing cordiality (and a couple of glasses of chambertin) soon put him at his ease and woke up his dormant appetite; which was a very large one, poor fellow!
He was told all about Little Billee’s death, and deeply moved to hear the cause which had brought it about, and then they talked of Trilby.
He pulled her watch out of his waistcoat-pocket and reverently kissed it, exclaiming: “*Ah! c’était un ange! un ange du Paradis!

他把便条折好递给引座员——请他转交“首席小提琴手——就是那位满头白发的先生”。
不多时，他看见盖科接过了便条，读完后低头沉思了良久。
随后盖科举目环顾整座剧场，塔菲随即在包厢里挥动白手帕，迎上了首席小提琴手的视线，只见后者欣然“朝上方微微颔首示意”。
第一幕刚一落幕，韦恩夫妇便抽身离席离开了剧场；塔菲向妻子低语解释了缘由，而布兰奇也早已急不可耐想离开，因为尽管她尚未完全参透那位活泼的坎塔丽迪夫人台词里的玄机，心头却已莫名感到隐隐不适。
他们来到了英国咖啡馆，在俯瞰林荫大道的夹层预订了一间温馨雅致的小包厢，点了一桌精美可口的宵夜：几样绝妙的野味炖肉、龙虾蛋黄酱冷盘，以及一两样更具特色的珍馐佳肴——配以最顶级的香贝丹红葡萄酒。每逢度假，塔菲在饮食讲究上向来极其挑剔考究，且从不计较花销。波尔多斯向来好客豪爽，喜好丰盛美味的佳肴；而阿多斯更是不折不扣的美酒鉴赏家！
点完餐后，他们信步走到大歌剧院旁和平咖啡馆门外的林荫大道小圆桌旁坐下，那里永远是巴黎最繁华喧嚣的景致；他们一边打量着巴黎的世相百态，一边悠然酝酿着胃口等候宵夜时分。
夜里十一点半，盖科如约而至——神色显得极为温顺谦卑。他看上去老态龙钟——比他的实际岁数至少苍老了十岁——身躯佝偻，仿佛一生饱经风霜磨难，历尽了尘世漫长而严酷的艰辛挣扎。
他亲吻了塔菲太太的手背，甚至巴不得连塔菲的手也一并亲吻，重逢的喜悦以及受邀共进宵夜的感激，教他感动得眼眶泛红、老泪纵横。他的举止温柔、依恋、充满讨人欢心的亲昵，活像一只忠诚可爱的小狗，能在一瞬间把你变成他的知心挚友。他显然真纯赤诚至极，一如既往地天真无邪得令人心酸动容。
起初由于神经过度激动，他几乎难以下咽；但在塔菲大快朵颐的表率与塔菲太太和蔼可亲、平易近人的热情款待下（外加两杯香贝丹美酒下肚），他很快便放松了下来，体内沉睡的食欲被彻底唤醒了；可怜的老人，胃口居然大得惊人！
他们向他据实相告了小比利的辞世，当听闻导致悲剧的病因真相时，盖科深受触动，随后大家的话题不可避免地转到了特里尔比身上。
他从背心口袋里掏出特里尔比留给他的那只金表，虔诚地印上一吻，失声叹道：“啊！她曾是一位天使！天堂里的天使！

“而且正是她一个人支撑着我们全家的生计——有时甚至让我们过上了豪奢璀璨的富贵日子！”

“啊，是的！然而你们知道，这一切全都是斯文加利造就的。斯文加利是我平生所见过的最伟大的艺术家！先生，斯文加利是个恶魔，是个大巫师！我当年曾把他奉若神明！是他当年在街头发现了我为几个铜板卖艺，是他一把拉住了我的手，成了我唯一的挚友，手把手教会了我平生所知晓的一切本事——然而他自己甚至根本不会拉小提琴！
“而如今他死了，连我自己也把怎么拉琴忘得一干二净了！英国的那座大牢！彻底摧垮了我，永远把我毁了！唉！那简直是人间地狱，去他妈的上帝（夫人，请恕我口出秽语）！如今我只配在‘西班牙苍蝇’剧场拉拉伴奏，给老坎塔丽迪唱那些下流歌伴奏，

“It does not want much of an obbligato, *hein*, a song so noble and so beautiful as that!
“And that song, monsieur, all Paris is singing it now. And that is the Paris that went mad when Trilby sang the ‘Nussbaum’ of Schumann at the Salle des Bashibazoucks. You heard her? Well!”
And here poor Gecko tried to laugh a little sardonic laugh in falsetto, like Svengali’s, full of scorn and bitterness﻿—and very nearly succeeded.
“But what made you strike him with﻿—with that knife, you know?”
“Ah, monsieur, it had been coming on for a long time. He used to work Trilby too hard; it was killing her﻿—it killed her at last! And then at the end he was unkind to her and scolded her and called her names﻿—horrid names﻿—and then one day in London he struck her. He struck her on the fingers with his baton, and she fell down on her knees and cried﻿ ﻿…
“Monsieur, I would have defended Trilby against a locomotive going *grande vitesse*! against my own father﻿—against the Emperor of Austria﻿—against the Pope! and I am a good Catholic, monsieur! I would have gone to the scaffold for her, and to the devil after!”
And he piously crossed himself.
“But, Svengali﻿—wasn’t *he* very fond of her?”
“Oh yes, monsieur! *quant à ça*, passionately! But she did not love him as he wished to be loved. She loved Litrebili, monsieur! Litrebili, the brother of madame. And I suppose that Svengali grew angry and jealous at last. He changed as soon as he came to Paris. Perhaps Paris reminded him of Litrebili﻿—and reminded Trilby, too!”
“But how on earth did Svengali ever manage to teach her how to sing like that? She had no ear for music whatever when *we* knew her!”
Gecko was silent for a while, and Taffy filled his glass, and gave him a cigar, and lit one himself.
“Monsieur, no﻿—that is true. She had not much ear. But she had such a voice as had never been heard. Svengali knew that. He had found it out long ago. Litolff had found it out, too. One day Svengali heard Litolff tell Meyerbeer that the most beautiful female voice in Europe belonged to an English grisette who sat as a model to sculptors in the quartier latin, but that unfortunately she was quite tone-deaf, and couldn’t sing one single note in tune. Imagine how Svengali chuckled! I see it from here!
“Well, we both taught her together﻿—for three years﻿—morning, noon, and night﻿—six﻿—eight hours a day.

“这样一首崇高纯洁的歌谣，倒也用不着什么高深的伴奏，是不是？
“先生，这首歌如今整个巴黎都在传唱呢。而当年在巴希巴祖克演艺厅特里尔比演唱舒曼《胡桃树》时为之发狂的，偏偏也是这个巴黎。当年您亲耳听过她的歌喉，对吧？好吧！”
说到这里，可怜的盖科学着斯文加利昔日的模样，用假声发出了一阵尖细刻毒的讽刺冷笑，满含着蔑视与辛酸——模仿得惟妙惟肖。
“不过，当年你究竟为什么要在剧院用——用那把刀子刺他呢？”
“啊，先生，这场怨恨其实郁积已久了。他把特里尔比折磨得太苦太狠了；那无休止的压榨正在要她的命——到头来当真害死了她！而且到最后，他待她暴虐残酷，动辄破口大骂，肆意侮辱谩骂她。”

It used to split me the heart to see her worked like that! We took her voice note by note﻿—there was no end to her notes, each more beautiful than the other﻿—velvet and gold, beautiful flowers, pearls, diamonds, rubies﻿—drops of dew and honey; peaches, oranges, and lemons! *en veux-tu en voilà!*﻿—all the perfumes and spices of the Garden of Eden! Svengali with his little flexible flageolet, I with my violin﻿—that is how we taught her to make the sounds﻿—and then how to use them. She was a *phénomène*, monsieur! She could keep on one note and make it go through all the colors in the rainbow﻿—according to the way Svengali looked at her. It would make you laugh﻿—it would make you cry﻿—but, cry or laugh, it was the sweetest, the most touching, the most beautiful note you ever heard﻿—except all her others! and each had as many overtones as the bells in the Carillon de Notre Dame. She could run up and down the scales, chromatic scales, quicker and better and smoother than Svengali on the piano, and more in tune than any piano! and her shake﻿—*ach!* twin stars, monsieur! She was the greatest contralto, the greatest soprano the world has ever known! the like of her has never been! the like of her will never be again! and yet she only sang in public for two years.
“*Ach!* those breaks and runs and sudden leaps from darkness into light and back again﻿—from earth to heaven!﻿ ﻿… those slurs and swoops and slides à la Paganini from one note to another, like a swallow flying!﻿ ﻿… or a gull! Do you remember them? how they drove you mad? Let any other singer in the world try to imitate them﻿—they would make you sick! That was Svengali﻿ ﻿… he was a magician!
“And how she looked, singing! do you remember? her hands behind her﻿—her dear, sweet, slender foot on a little stool﻿—her thick hair lying down all along her back! And that good smile like the Madonna’s so soft and bright and kind! *Ach!* *Bel ucel di Dio!* it was to make you weep for love, merely to see her (*c’était à vous faire pleurer d’amour, rien que de la voir*)! That was Trilby! Nightingale and bird-of-paradise in one!

每每眼看着她被那样残酷操弄，我的心都快要撕裂了！我们当年是一个音符一个音符地调教她的嗓音——她喉咙里的音符仿佛取之不尽用之不竭，一个比一个绝美动人——如同天鹅绒与黄金，如同绝美的繁花、珍珠、钻石与红宝石——宛若甘露与纯蜜的滴落；鲜桃、甜橙与柠檬！应有尽有，享用不尽！——饱含着伊甸园里所有的芬芳与香料！斯文加利吹奏着他那支灵巧的小短笛，我拉着我的小提琴——我们就是这般手把手教会她发出那些天籁之音——继而教会她如何自如驾驭。先生，她是个旷世罕见的音乐奇才！她能把同一个音符拖长，随心所欲让它幻化出彩虹般的斑斓色彩——全凭斯文加利当时如何用眼神指引她。那声音能教你捧腹开怀——也能教你痛哭流涕——但不论是哭是笑，那都是你平生听过的最甜美、最动人、最纯净的音符——除了她唱出的其余音符之外！每一个音符所蕴含的泛音，比巴黎圣母院钟楼上的编钟还要繁复丰满。她能把音阶、半音阶跑得比斯文加利在钢琴上弹奏得还要迅捷、纯美与流畅，音准比世间任何一台名琴都要精准无误！还有她的颤音——天哪！简直是双星交相辉映，先生！她是有史以来全世界最伟大的女低音，亦是最绝顶的女高音！前无古人，后无来者！然而她在公众舞台上登台的时间，总共仅仅只有两年！
“唉！那些宛若帕格尼尼神技般的顿挫、琶音，那些从深渊骤然跃向光明、又跌落而下的跌宕起伏——从凡尘直插天国！……那些从一个音符滑向另一个音符的滑音与俯冲，宛若飞燕掠波！……又宛如海鸥翱翔！难道你们不记得了吗？当年那些乐章是何等教人如痴如狂！哪怕让全世界任何其他歌唱家妄图去模仿，都会让人恶心欲呕！那全都是斯文加利的魔力……他是个真正的大魔法师！
“还有她歌唱时的仪态风姿！难道你们不记得了吗？双手交叠背在身后——她那娇小、动人、修长的玉足踏在一张小脚凳上——浓密的秀发如瀑布般披散在后背！还有那宛如圣母玛利亚般圣洁慈祥、温柔明朗的微笑！天哪！上帝创造的美妙百灵鸟！光是看她一眼，就足以教人因深情爱慕而泪流满面！那正是特里尔比！集夜莺与极乐鸟于一身的化身！
“总之只要斯文加利向她点拨示范过，她便能随心所欲驾驭任何乐章、发出任何声音——而斯文加利是有史以来最登峰造极的音乐宗师！一旦她掌握了一门绝技，便终生融会贯通。事情就是这样！”

“How strange,” said Taffy, “that she should have suddenly gone out of her senses that night at Drury Lane, and so completely forgotten it all! I suppose she saw Svengali die in the box opposite, and that drove her mad!”
And then Taffy told the little fiddler about Trilby’s death-song, like a swan’s, and Svengali’s photograph. But Gecko had heard it all from Marta, who was now dead.
Gecko sat and smoked and pondered for a while, and looked from one to the other. Then he pulled himself together with an effort, so to speak, and said, “Monsieur, she never went mad﻿—not for one moment!”
“What! Do you mean to say she *deceived* us all?”
“*Non, monsieur!* She could never deceive anybody, and never would. *She had forgotten﻿—*voilà tout!**”
“But hang it all, my friend, one doesn’t *forget* such a﻿—”
“Monsieur, listen! She is dead. And Svengali is dead﻿—and Marta also. And I have a good little malady that will kill me soon, *Gott sei dank*﻿—and without much pain.
“I will tell you a secret.
“*There were two Trilbys.* There was the Trilby you knew, who could not sing one single note in tune. She was an angel of paradise. She is now! But she had no more idea of singing than I have of winning a steeplechase at the croix de Berny. She could no more sing than a fiddle can play itself! She could never tell one tune from another﻿—one note from the next. Do you remember how she tried to sing ‘Ben Bolt’ that day when she first came to the studio in the Place St. Anatole des Arts? It was droll, *hein? à se boucher les oreilles*! Well, that was Trilby, your Trilby! that was my Trilby too﻿—and I loved her as one loves an only love, an only sister, an only child﻿—a gentle martyr on earth, a blessed saint in heaven! And that Trilby was enough for *me*!
“And that was the Trilby that loved your brother, madame﻿—oh! but with all the love that was in her! He did not know what he had lost, your brother! Her love, it was immense, like her voice, and just as full of celestial sweetness and sympathy! She told me everything! *ce pauvre Litrebili, ce qu’il a perdu!*
“But all at once﻿—*pr‑r‑rout*! presto! *augenblick!*﻿ ﻿… with one wave of his hand over her﻿—with one look of his eye﻿—with a word﻿—Svengali could turn her into the other Trilby, *his* Trilby, and make her do whatever he liked﻿ ﻿… you might have run a red-hot needle into her and she would not have felt it.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

“这可真是太不可思议了，”塔菲感慨道，“在那天德鲁里巷剧院的首演之夜，她怎么会突然神智失常，将过往的一切忘得一干二净呢！我想，八成是她亲眼看见斯文加利死在对面的包厢里，那一幕把她活活吓疯了！”
随后塔菲向这位瘦小的小提琴手详细讲述了特里尔比临终前宛如天鹅绝唱般的一幕，以及那张神秘寄达的斯文加利肖像照片。不过盖科早已从如今已然过世的玛塔口中知晓了全部原委。
盖科坐在那里抽着烟沉思良久，目光在塔菲夫妇二人身上流转。随后他仿佛竭尽全力打起精神，深吸了一口气说道：“先生，她从来没有发过疯——哪怕一秒钟都没有！”
“什么？！你的意思是说，她当年一直在欺骗我们所有人？”
“不，先生！她绝不可能欺瞒任何人，也绝不会撒谎。她是真的彻底忘得一干二净了——仅此而已！”
“可去他妈的，我的朋友，一个人怎么可能把这等大事忘得——”
“先生，听我说！她如今已经入土为安了。斯文加利死了——玛塔也过世了。而我身上也落下了要命的沉疴，托上帝的福，用不了多久我也会平静死去，且不会遭太多罪。
“我告诉你们一个天大的秘密吧。
“这世上曾经存在过两个特里尔比。一个是你们所熟识的那个特里尔比，五音不全，连一个音符都唱不准。她是天堂里的天使。如今她依然是！但她对声乐的窍门一窍不通，正如我根本无法在贝尔尼十字赛马场赢得越野障碍赛马冠军一样。她自己根本不会唱歌，就像一把小提琴自己根本不会拉奏出乐声一样！她连一首曲调同另一首曲调、一个音符同下一个音符都分不清。难道你们忘记了当年她头一回踏进圣阿纳托尔艺术广场画室时试唱《本·博尔特》的滑稽模样了吗？滑稽透顶，是不是？简直教人想捂住耳朵！然而那正是特里尔比，你们的特里尔比！那也是我的特里尔比——我爱她，正如一个人深爱着世间唯一的挚爱、唯一的胞妹、唯一的骨肉一样——在人间，她是一位温柔纯洁的殉道圣徒，在天国，她是一位受人敬仰的圣洁天使！对我而言，拥有那个特里尔比就足够了！
“夫人，那也正是当年全心全意深爱着令兄的特里尔比啊——噢！倾尽了她体内全部的深情！令兄当年根本不知道自己究竟失去了什么！她心中的爱是何等浩瀚无垠，宛若她那副天籁之音一样广袤，同样充盈着天堂般的温柔与共鸣！她当年把心事全都倾诉给我听了！可怜的小比利，他究竟失去了何等无价的至宝啊！
“然而只要斯文加利的手指在她眼前轻轻一划——只要他的眼神猛然一凝——只要从他嘴里吐出一句暗语——斯文加利就能在眨眼之间，把她彻底变成另一个特里尔比，变成属于他斯文加利自己的特里尔比，任凭他随意驱使摆布……哪怕你拿一根烧得通红的滚烫铁针扎进她的皮肉里，她也绝不会感到半点疼痛。……”

===Original===

“He had but to say ‘*Dors!*’ and she suddenly became an unconscious Trilby of marble, who could produce wonderful sounds﻿—just the sounds he wanted, and nothing else﻿—and think his thoughts and wish his wishes﻿—and love him at his bidding with a strange unreal factitious love﻿ ﻿… just his own love for himself turned inside out﻿—*à l’envers*﻿—and reflected back on him, as from a mirror﻿ ﻿… *un écho, un simulacre, quoi! pas autre chose!*﻿ ﻿… It was not worth having! I was not even jealous!
“Well, that was the Trilby he taught how to sing﻿—and﻿—and I helped him, God of heaven forgive me! That Trilby was just a singing-machine﻿—an organ to play upon﻿—an instrument of music﻿—a Stradivarius﻿—a flexible flageolet of flesh and blood﻿—a voice, and nothing more﻿—just the unconscious voice that Svengali sang with﻿—for it takes two to sing like la Svengali, monsieur﻿—the one who has got the voice, and the one who knows what to do with it.﻿ ﻿… So that when you heard her sing the ‘Nussbaum,’ the ‘Impromptu,’ you heard Svengali singing with her voice, just as you hear Joachim play a chaconne of Bach with his fiddle!﻿ ﻿… Herr Joachim’s fiddle﻿ ﻿… what does it know of Sebastian Bach? and as for chaconnes﻿ ﻿… *il s’en moque pas mal, ce fameux violon!*﻿ ﻿…
“And *our* Trilby﻿ ﻿… what did she know of Schumann, Chopin?﻿—nothing at all! She mocked herself not badly of Nussbaums and impromptus﻿ ﻿… they would make her yawn to demantibulate her jaws!﻿ ﻿… When Svengali’s Trilby was being taught to sing﻿ ﻿… when Svengali’s Trilby was singing﻿—or seemed to *you* as if she were singing﻿—*our* Trilby had ceased to exist﻿ ﻿… *our* Trilby was fast asleep﻿ ﻿… in fact, *our* Trilby was *dead*.﻿ ﻿…
“Ah, monsieur﻿ ﻿… that Trilby of Svengali’s! I have heard her sing to kings and queens in royal palaces!﻿ ﻿… as no woman has ever sung before or since.﻿ ﻿… I have seen emperors and grand-dukes kiss her hand, monsieur﻿—and their wives and daughters kiss her lips, and weep.﻿ ﻿…
“I have seen the horses taken out of her sledge and the pick of the nobility drag her home to the hotel﻿ ﻿… with torchlights and choruses and shoutings of glory and long life to her!﻿ ﻿… and serenades all night, under her window!﻿ ﻿… *she* never knew! she heard nothing﻿—felt nothing﻿—saw nothing! and she bowed to them, right and left, like a queen!

===Chinese===

“他只需口中轻念一声‘睡吧！’，她便会刹那间化作一个浑然无知的汉白玉特里尔比雕像，能够发出种种不可思议的天籁之音——分毫不差全是斯文加利所期望的声音，丝毫不带杂质——脑海中思考着他的思想，渴望着他的渴望——并在他的意旨驱使下，怀着一种奇异、虚幻且人为捏造的爱意去‘爱’他……那不过是他自身自恋的情欲被整个翻转过来，如同一面镜子般折射回他自己身上……纯属一种回声、一个幻影、一个替身！除此以外什么都不是！那份所谓的爱根本不值一提！我甚至连一丝妒忌都产生不起来！
“不错，那正是他悉心调教如何歌唱的特里尔比——而且——而且我也在一旁充当了帮凶，愿天上的天主宽恕我的罪孽！那个特里尔比不过是一具歌唱机器——是一台供人弹奏的风琴——是一件乐器——是一把斯特拉迪瓦里名琴——是一支有血有肉的灵巧活短笛——一副肉体嗓音，除此再无其他——纯粹是斯文加利用以借体发声的浑然不觉之音——因为先生，想要唱出斯文加利夫人那般的奇迹，必须两个人合二为一：一个拥有无双的嗓子，另一个则懂得如何驱使这副嗓子。……因此，当你们听到她演唱《胡桃树》、演唱肖邦的《即兴曲》时，你们听到的实际上是斯文加利用她的肉嗓在引吭高歌，正如你们聆听约阿希姆用他的小提琴拉奏巴赫的恰空舞曲一样！……约阿希姆先生的小提琴……对塞巴斯蒂安·巴赫懂些什么呢？至于那些高深的恰空舞曲……那把名琴本身根本不屑一顾！
“而我们那个特里尔比……她对舒曼、对肖邦又懂得些什么呢？一无所知！她对什么胡桃树、即兴曲向来嗤之以鼻……听得直打哈欠，下巴都快脱臼了！……每当斯文加利的特里尔比在接受调教歌唱时……每当斯文加利的特里尔比引吭高歌——或者在你们看来仿佛是她在歌唱时——我们那个真正的特里尔比早已不复存在了……我们那个特里尔比早已沉沉熟睡……事实上，我们那个特里尔比在那一刻早已‘死去’了。……
“啊，先生……斯文加利手下的那个特里尔比！我曾亲耳听她在金碧辉煌的皇家宫殿里为国王与王后献艺！……唱得那般绝妙，世间前无古人，后无来者。……我曾亲眼目睹皇帝与大公们亲吻她的手背，先生——他们的后妃与公主们更是亲吻她的双唇，感动得泪落如雨。……
“我曾亲眼目睹狂热的人群解开她雪橇上的骏马，最显赫尊贵的贵族名流争先恐后亲自为她拉车护送回旅馆……沿途火把通明，合唱齐鸣，欢呼着她的荣光，祝愿她万寿无疆！……整夜整夜的人群聚集在她的窗下为她弹唱小夜曲！……可她自己对此一无所知！她听不见任何声音——感觉不到任何触碰——看不见眼前的一切！她只是像一位真正的女王般，机械而尊贵地向左右观众欠身颔首！”

===Original===

“I have played the fiddle for her while she sang in the streets, at fairs and festas and Kermessen﻿ ﻿… and seen the people go mad to hear her﻿ ﻿… and once, at Prague, Svengali fell down in a fit from sheer excitement! and then, suddenly, *our* Trilby woke up and wondered what it was all about﻿ ﻿… and we took him home and put him to bed and left him with Marta﻿—and Trilby and I went together arm in arm all over the town to fetch a doctor and buy things for supper﻿—and that was the happiest hour in all my life!
“*Ach!* what an existence! what travels! what triumphs! what adventures! Things to fill a book﻿—a dozen books﻿—Those five happy years﻿—with those two Trilbys! what recollections!﻿ ﻿… I think of nothing else, night or day﻿ ﻿… even as I play the fiddle for old Cantharidi. *Ach!*﻿ ﻿… To think how often I have played the fiddle for la Svengali﻿ ﻿… to have done that is to have lived﻿ ﻿… and then to come home to Trilby﻿ ﻿… *our* Trilby﻿ ﻿… the *real* Trilby!﻿ ﻿… *Gott sei dank! Ich habe *geliebt und gelebet! geliebt und gelebet! geliebt und gelebet!** *Cristo di Dio*.﻿ ﻿… Sweet sister in heaven.﻿ ﻿… *Ô Dieu de Misère, ayez pitié de nous.*﻿ ﻿…”
---
His eyes were red, and his voice was high and shrill and tremulous and full of tears; these remembrances were too much for him; and perhaps also the chambertin! He put his elbows on the table and hid his face in his hands and wept, muttering to himself in his own language (Whatever that might have been﻿—Polish, probably) as if he were praying.
Taffy and his wife got up and leaned on the window-bar and looked out on the deserted boulevards, where an army of scavengers, noiseless and taciturn, was cleansing the asphalt roadway. The night above was dark, but “star-dials hinted of morn,” and a fresh breeze had sprung up, making the leaves dance and rustle on the sycamore-trees along the Boulevard﻿—a nice little breeze; just the sort of little breeze to do Paris good. A four-wheel cab came by at a foot pace, the driver humming a tune; Taffy hailed him; he said, “*V’là, m’sieur!*” and drew up.
Taffy rang the bell, and asked for the bill, and paid it. Gecko had apparently fallen asleep. Taffy gently woke him up, and told him how late it was. The poor little man seemed dazed and rather tipsy, and looked older than ever; sixty, seventy﻿—any age you like.

===Chinese===

“我曾为她在街头卖艺时伴奏拉琴，在各个乡村集市、庆典节日和游乐集会上……眼看着成千上万的人群为她的歌声如痴如狂……有一回在布拉格，斯文加利因过度狂喜兴奋当场发作羊角风昏死过去！就在那一瞬间，属于我们真正的特里尔比骤然苏醒了过来，茫然不知所措地环顾四周，纳闷这究竟是怎么一回事……我们合力把他抬回家安顿在床上，留给玛塔照料——随后特里尔比同我两个人手挽着手，走遍了全城去请医生、购买做宵夜的吃食——那是我平生度过的最幸福的一刻钟！
“唉！那是何等波澜壮阔的生涯啊！何等漫长的旅程！何等辉煌的凯旋！何等神奇的历险！那些经历足以写满一整本书——乃至十几本书——那同两个特里尔比共同度过的五年幸福时光！那是何等珍贵的回忆啊！……无论白天黑夜，我脑子里想的唯有这些……哪怕此刻在剧场给老坎塔丽迪拉琴伴奏时也不例外。唉！……回想起当年我曾无数次为斯文加利夫人拉琴伴奏……能亲历这一切，才算是不枉活此生啊……随后又能回到特里尔比身边……属于我们大家的特里尔比……那个真正的特里尔比！……感谢上帝！我曾深爱过，也曾真正活过！我曾深爱过，也曾真正活过！我曾深爱过，也曾真正活过！天主基督在上。……天国里温柔纯洁的妹妹啊。……受难的天主啊，请怜悯我们吧。……”
---
盖科的一双眸子通红充血，嗓音变得高亢、尖锐、颤抖且饱含着老泪；这些汹涌的记忆对他而言实在太过沉重难以承受；或许那几杯顶级香贝丹红葡萄酒也起了催化之力！他把双肘撑在餐桌上，双手掩面放声痛哭，嘴里操着他那本民族的母语（不论究竟是波兰语还是别的什么）喃喃自语，仿佛正在虔诚祈祷。
塔菲和妻子双双站起身，倚靠在窗边的铁栏杆前，凝视着窗外寂静无人、空空荡荡的林荫大道，只见一支沉默寡言的清道夫大军正悄无声息地清扫着沥青路面。头顶的夜空昏黑深沉，然而“星斗的指针已暗透破晓的曙光”，清冽的晨风悄然拂起，吹得林荫道两侧悬铃木上的枝叶沙沙作响、翩翩起舞——这是一阵舒爽怡人的微风；正是那种能让整座巴黎神清气爽的绝妙晨风。一辆四轮出租马车迈着从容的碎步从街心缓缓驶过，车夫嘴里哼着小曲；塔菲招手叫车；车夫应道：“这就来，先生！”随即稳稳拉紧缰绳停靠在路边。
塔菲拉响门铃要来账单，结清了餐费。盖科此刻似乎已沉沉睡去。塔菲轻轻将他唤醒，温和地提醒他夜色已深。可怜的小老头显得神情恍惚、略带醉意，看上去比以往任何时候都更加苍老；六十岁、七十岁——任凭你怎么猜都不为过。

===Original===

Taffy helped him on with his greatcoat, and, taking him by the arm, led him downstairs, giving him his card, and telling him how glad he was to have seen him, and that he would write to him from England﻿—a promise which was kept, one may be sure.
Gecko uncovered his fuzzy white head, and took Mrs. Taffy’s hand and kissed it, and thanked her warmly for her *si bon et sympathique accueil*.
Then Taffy all but lifted him into the cab, the jolly cabman saying:
“*Ah! bon﻿—connais bien, celui là; vous savez﻿—c’est lui qui joue du violon aux Mouches d’Espagne! Il a soupé, l’bourgeois; n’est-ce pas, m’sieur? ‘petits bonheurs de contrebande,’ hein?﻿ ﻿… ayez pas peur! on vous aura soin de lui! il joue joliment bien, m’sieur; n’est-ce pas?*”
Taffy shook Gecko’s hand, and asked,
“*Où restez-vous, Gecko?*”
“*Quarante-huit, Rue des Pousse-cailloux, au cinquième.*”
“How strange!” said Taffy to his wife﻿—“how touching! why, that’s where Trilby used to live﻿—the very number! the very floor!”
“*Oui, oui*,” said Gecko, waking up; “*c’est l’ancienne mansarde à Trilby﻿—j’y suis depuis douze ans﻿—*j’y suis, j’y reste*.*﻿ ﻿…”
And he laughed feebly at his mild little joke.
Taffy told the address to the cabman, and gave him five francs.
“*Merci, m’sieur! C’est de l’aut’ côté de l’eau﻿—près de la Sorbonne, s’pas? On vous aura soin du bourgeois; soyez tranquille﻿—ayez pas peur! quarante-huit; on y va! Bonsoir, monsieur et dame!*” And he clacked his whip and rattled away, singing:

> “V’là mon mari qui r’garde﻿—

Prends garde!

Ne m’chatouill’ plus!”

Mr. and Mrs. Wynne walked back to the hotel, which was not far. She hung on to his big arm and crept close to him, and shivered a little. It was quite chilly. Their footsteps were very audible in the stillness; “pit-pat, flopety-clop,” otherwise they were both silent. They were tired, yawny, sleepy, and very sad; and each was thinking (and knew the other was thinking) that a week in Paris was just enough﻿—and how nice it would be, in just a few hours more, to hear the rooks cawing round their own quiet little English country home﻿—where three jolly boys would soon be coming for the holidays.
And there we will leave them to their useful, humdrum, happy domestic existence﻿—than which there is no better that I know of, at their time of life﻿—and no better time of life than theirs!

> “Où peut-on être mieux qu’au sein de ta famille?”

===Chinese===

塔菲替他穿上宽大的厚呢大衣，挽着他的臂膀领他走下楼梯，递给他自己的名片，告诉他今夜能与他重逢是何等欣慰，并答应回到英国后定会常给他写信——世人尽可放心，这诺言后来被不折不扣地践行了。
盖科脱下帽子，露出一头毛茸茸的蓬松白发，拉起塔菲太太的手虔诚印上一吻，由衷感激她今夜“如此温存深切的款待与垂青”。
随后塔菲几乎是像抱孩子一样将他轻轻托送进了马车车厢，那位生性快活的车夫打趣道：
“啊哈！好嘞——这位老主顾我认得；您知道——就是在‘西班牙苍蝇’剧场拉小提琴的那位爷嘛！今晚宵夜吃得挺美吧，东家；是不是，先生？这出《走私来的小确幸》可真逗乐，对吧？……您二位放心！我准把这位爷稳稳当当送回顾头！他老人家的琴拉得可真是俊极了，先生；可不是吗？”
塔菲握着盖科的手，探询道：
“盖科，你如今住在何处？”
“石子路街四十八号，五楼顶层。”
“真是太不可思议了！”塔菲转过头动容地对妻子轻叹道——“多么叫人动容啊！那正是当年特里尔比住过的地方——连门牌号都一模一样！连楼层都分毫不差！”
“对，对，”盖科仿佛大梦初醒般微笑道，“正是特里尔比当年的旧阁楼——我在那儿已经住了整整十二年了——我既已住在那儿，便会长留在那儿。……”
说着，他为自己这句淡淡的温和俏皮话虚弱地笑了起来。
塔菲向车夫报了地址，随手赏了他一枚五法郎银币。
“多谢先生！是在塞纳河对岸——靠近索邦大学那边，对吧？您放一百个心，我定把这位老爷子妥妥帖帖伺候到家；踏踏实实的——别担心！四十八号；这就走着！先生、太太，晚安！”说着车夫响亮地一甩马鞭，马蹄声嘚嘚远去，夜风中飘来他粗犷的哼唱：

> “瞧我那当家的正瞪着眼瞅呢——
小心点！
别再抓老娘的痒痒肉！”

韦恩夫妇挽着手走回下榻的旅馆，路途并不遥远。布兰奇紧紧依偎在丈夫魁伟结实的臂弯里，身子微微瑟缩着。夜风确实颇有些寒意。在四下的死寂中，两人的脚步声格外清晰可闻；“啪嗒、啪嗒，吧嗒、吧嗒”，除此以外两人皆默默无语。他们感到疲惫、哈欠连天、昏昏欲睡，心中却充溢着深沉的感伤；他们各自都在心头暗想（而且深知对方亦在作如是想）：在巴黎度过的一周时光已然足够圆满了——仅仅再过数个小时，便能在他们位于英格兰宁静乡间的清幽寓所旁，聆听秃鼻乌鸦在林间的啼鸣，那该是何等温馨惬意啊——在那里，三个活泼快活的儿子马上就要放假归家承欢膝下了。
在此，我们不妨就此让这对璧人回归他们有益、平凡而美满的家庭生活吧——就他们所处的人生岁月而言，据我所知世间再无任何生活比这更美好幸福——而人生的岁月，亦再无任何时期比他们此刻更为美妙圆满！

> “世间何处能比家庭的怀抱更为温馨？”

===Original===

That blessed harbor of refuge well within our reach, and having really cut our wisdom teeth at last, and learned the ropes, and left off hankering after the moon﻿—we can do with so little down here.﻿ ﻿…

> A little work, a little play

To keep us going﻿—and so, good day!
> A little warmth, a little light

Of love’s bestowing﻿—and so, good night!
> A little fun, to match the sorrow

Of each day’s growing﻿—and so, good-morrow!
> A little trust that when we die

We reap our sowing! And so﻿—goodbye!

===Chinese===

那座神圣宁静的避风港湾就在我们触手可及之处，历经沧桑之后，我们终于长全了智慧的牙齿，参透了人世的玄机，不再执迷于伸手揽月的心妄——在这凡尘俗世间，我们所求的不过是这般微薄的一点点寄托。……

> 些许劳作，些许欢愉，
支撑我们度过此生——就此，日安！
> 些许温存，些许微光，
皆由深情所赐予——就此，晚安！
> 些许幽默，以抵御
日常滋长的哀伤——就此，明朝再见！
> 些许笃信：待到我们阖眼离世，
今生播撒之种终将迎来丰收！就此——永别重逢！

## Endnotes / 尾注
===Original===

1. Glossary.﻿—*Pioupiou* (alias *pousse-caillou*, alias *tourlourou*)﻿—a private soldier of the line. *Zouzou*﻿—a Zouave. *Nounou*﻿—a wet-nurse with a pretty ribboned cap and long streamers. *Toutou*﻿—a nondescript French lapdog, of no breed known to Englishmen (a regular little beast!) *Loulou*﻿—a Pomeranian dog﻿—not much better. ↩︎

===Chinese===

1. 词汇表。——*Pioupiou*（亦称 *pousse-caillou*，亦称 *tourlourou*）——步兵线列步兵大兵。*Zouzou*——祖阿夫轻步兵。*Nounou*——戴着饰有漂亮丝带和长飘带便帽的奶妈。*Toutou*——一种难以名状的法国哈巴狗，英国人从未见识过的杂种犬（纯粹是一只招人嫌的小畜生！）。*Loulou*——博美犬——也没好到哪儿去。↩︎
